After about three weeks of this pattern affecting his ability to stay focused on the CCTV monitors and increasing arguments with his wife about his \"zoning out,\" he grew worried that his mental state was putting both his job performance and marriage at risk, prompting him to schedule an appointment with a psychiatrist recommended by his primary care doctor.\n- Chief Complaint: “My mind feels foggy all the time—I can’t focus at work or even finish a simple puzzle like I used to.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Lately I walk around feeling this heavy, empty sort of sadness, like I’m just going through the motions at home and at work, and sometimes I catch myself tearing up for no real reason when I’m alone in the break room or driving home in the morning.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to look forward to cooking dinner with my wife or messing around on the guitar with my kid, but now even on my nights off I just sit on the couch staring at the TV and turn them down when they ask me to join in because I just don’t feel like doing anything.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): Most days I’m just not hungry, so I either skip meals at work or pick at whatever’s there, and my wife’s been saying my uniform looks a bit looser around the waist lately.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): My body feels like it’s moving through mud—walking my rounds on the floor I’m slower than usual, and even just getting up from the chair in the control room takes an extra push, so I end up sitting there staring at the screens longer than I should.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): It’s like my brain is full of cotton; I lose track of what camera I’m supposed to be watching, I have to reread the same line of a crossword three or four times, and even simple things like deciding what to make for breakfast can leave me standing in the kitchen just stuck.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but when I’m driving home after a long shift I catch myself thinking it wouldn’t really matter if I didn’t wake up or if something happened to me, because everyone might be better off without me messing things up.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Because of how foggy and slowed down I feel, my supervisor has pulled me aside twice about missing things on the monitors, and at home my wife is upset that I just sit there and don’t engage with her or our kid like I used to, so it feels like both my job and my marriage are being strained.\n- duration: These problems have been going on for about three weeks now, ever since that especially busy month at the casino when the tension at home really flared up.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. I do worry a lot about letting my family down or messing things up for them, though.\n\nDoctor: During this difficult time, have you had any thoughts of death, hurting yourself, or feeling like life is no longer worth living?\n\nPatient: I don't have any plan to hurt myself, but I do get those thoughts sometimes. When I'm driving home after a long shift, I catch myself thinking it wouldn't really matter if I didn't wake up or if something happened to me. I just feel like my family might be better off without me messing things up.\n\nDoctor: How has your appetite been lately? I don't feel restless at all; it's really just a heavy, physical slowness.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of low mood, sadness, or loss of interest like this at any other point in your life, or is this the very first time?\n\nPatient: This is the very first time I've ever felt like this. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
